The test test_render_marks_last_row_as_total expects it to support mark_last=True, which suggests that render_rows should have that functionality. But maybe that test is legacy or incorrect. Alternatively, the totals row is not supposed to be marked as total by render_rows; instead, the export function should just render the totals row as normal. The test_export_appends_totals_row expects the totals row to be \"TOTAL,4,6\", which is just a normal row with \"TOTAL\" in first column. So we don't need mark_last functionality. The test_render_marks_last_row_as_total might be obsolete or wrong.\n\nSimilarly, test_column_totals_ignores_text_cells expects column_totals to return [\"\", 3] for input [[\"a\", 1], [\"b\", 2]]. This suggests that column_totals should skip non-numeric values or treat them as 0. But the current column_totals just sums, causing TypeError when encountering string.\n\nWe have a few options:\n\n1. Fix column_totals to handle non-numeric values by skipping or converting? The test expects [\"\", 3], meaning for the first column which has strings \"a\" and \"b\", the total is empty string? Or maybe null? Actually [\"\", 3] means first column total is empty string (or zero?) and second column total is 3. This is odd.\n\n2. Fix render_rows to ignore the mark_last test or adjust it.\n\n3. Run the tests and see if our changes fix the export but break others. We might need to address all failing tests.\n\nBut the addendum says: \"Procedure: 1. Restate the task in one line. 2. Find the files: grep for the identifiers named in the task. If none are named, grep for the words in the task that look like code. 3. Read the matching regions, and the test that covers them. 4. Make the smallest change that completes the task. 5. Run the test command. 6.
